```
Output:
Explain provides human-readable context about sessions, commits, and checkpoints.

Use this command to understand what happened during agent-driven development,
either for self-review or to understand a teammate's work.

By default, shows checkpoints on the current branch. Pass a checkpoint ID or
commit SHA as a positional argument to explain a specific item, or use flags.

Viewing specific items:
  entire explain <id-or-sha>           Auto-detects checkpoint ID or commit SHA
  entire explain --checkpoint <id>     Force interpretation as checkpoint ID
  entire explain --commit <ref>        Force interpretation as commit ref

Filtering the list view:
  --session      Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix)

Output verbosity levels (when explaining a specific item):
  Default:         Detailed view with scoped prompts (ID, session, tokens, intent, prompts, files)
  --short          Summary only (ID, session, timestamp, tokens, intent)
  --full           Parsed full transcript (all prompts/responses from entire session)
  --raw-transcript Raw transcript file (JSONL format)

Machine-readable export modes (additive surface for external consumers):
  --json           Metadata-only JSON. Lists checkpoints when no target is given;
                   emits a single checkpoint envelope when a target is supplied.
                   Transcript bytes are NEVER embedded in the JSON envelope.
  --transcript     Stream stored checkpoint transcript bytes (JSONL) to stdout
                   for the selected session. Same bytes as --raw-transcript
                   while checkpoints v1 is the checkpoint store.
  --session-index  Pick a session within a multi-session checkpoint (0-based).
                   Defaults to the latest session. Only meaningful with
                   --transcript or --raw-transcript.
  --limit          Cap the number of checkpoints returned by the list view.
                   Defaults to 100. When the cap is hit, a stderr note
                   says how many were skipped. Only meaningful with --json.

Summary generation:
  --generate    Generate an AI summary for the checkpoint
  --force       Regenerate even if a summary already exists (requires --generate)

Performance options:
  --search-all  Remove branch/depth limits when searching for commits (may be slow)

Checkpoint detail view shows:
  - Author of the checkpoint
  - Associated git commits that reference the checkpoint
  - Prompts and responses from the session

Note: --session filters the list view; the positional arg, --commit, and --checkpoint are mutually exclusive.

Usage:
  entire checkpoint explain [checkpoint-id | commit-sha] [flags]

Flags:
  -c, --checkpoint string             Explain a specific checkpoint (ID or prefix)
      --commit string                 Explain a specific commit (SHA or ref, "commit-ish")
      --force                         Regenerate summary even if one already exists (requires --generate)
      --full                          Show full parsed transcript (all prompts/responses)
      --generate                      Generate an AI summary for the checkpoint
  -h, --help                          help for explain
      --json                          Output metadata as JSON (no transcript bytes)
      --limit int                     Cap the list view at N checkpoints (default: 100). Only meaningful with --json.
      --no-pager                      Disable pager output
      --raw-transcript                Show raw transcript file (JSONL format)
      --search-all                    Search all commits (no branch/depth limit, may be slow)
      --session string                Filter checkpoints by session ID (or prefix)
      --session-index int             Session index within a multi-session checkpoint (0-based, defaults to latest) (default -1)
  -s, --short                         Show summary only (omit prompts and files)
      --summary-timeout-seconds int   Hard deadline in seconds for --generate summary generation; overrides summary_timeout_seconds setting. 0 = use setting or 5m default.
      --transcript                    Stream stored checkpoint transcript bytes to stdout
```

```
branch:<name>, repo:<owner/name>, and repo:* to search all accessible repos.

      --json            Output as JSON

      --page int        Page number (1-based) (default 1)
      --repo string     Filter by repository (owner/name or *)
```

```
Enable Entire with session tracking for your AI agent workflows.

If Entire is not yet configured, this runs the full configuration flow.
If Entire is already configured but disabled, this re-enables it.

If the current directory is not a git repository, Entire can initialize one
for you and (optionally) create a matching GitHub repository via the gh CLI.

Usage:
  entire enable [flags]

Flags:
      --absolute-git-hook-path          Embed full binary path in git hooks (for GUI git clients that don't source shell profiles)
      --agent string                    Agent to set up hooks for (e.g., claude-code, codex, copilot-cli, cursor, factoryai-droid, gemini, opencode, pi; external agents on $PATH are also available). Enables non-interactive mode.
      --checkpoint-remote string        Checkpoint remote in provider:owner/repo format (e.g., github:org/checkpoints-repo)
  -f, --force                           Force reinstall hooks (removes existing Entire hooks first)
  -h, --help                            help for enable
      --init-repo                       If not a git repo, initialize one non-interactively
      --initial-commit-message string   Commit message for the initial commit when bootstrapping a new repo
      --local                           Write settings to .entire/settings.local.json instead of .entire/settings.json
      --no-github                       Initialize local git repo only; skip creating a GitHub remote
      --no-init-repo                    If not a git repo, exit instead of prompting to initialize one
      --project                         Write settings to .entire/settings.json even if it already exists
      --repo-name string                GitHub repository name for the new repo (used when bootstrapping)
      --repo-owner string               GitHub user or organization login for the new repo
      --repo-visibility string          GitHub repository visibility: public, private, or internal
      --skip-initial-commit             Don't create the initial commit when bootstrapping a new repo
      --skip-push-sessions              Disable automatic pushing of session logs on git push
      --telemetry                       Enable anonymous usage analytics (default true)
  -y, --yes                             Accept all defaults without prompting (in a non-repo directory: init git, create private GitHub repo, commit; then enable all agents and accept telemetry)
Update non-agent Entire settings in the current repository.

Manages telemetry, git-hook installation mode, strategy options, and summary
provider configuration. Agent installation is handled by 'entire agent'.

Examples:
  entire configure                                # Show this help
  entire configure --telemetry=false              # Opt out of telemetry
  entire configure --absolute-git-hook-path       # Reinstall git hook with absolute path
  entire configure --force                        # Reinstall git hook
  entire configure --checkpoint-remote github:org/checkpoints
  entire configure --summarize-provider claude-code
  entire configure --summarize-timeout-seconds 300   # 5m deadline for explain --generate

Usage:
  entire configure [flags]

Flags:
      --absolute-git-hook-path          Embed full binary path in git hooks (for GUI git clients that don't source shell profiles)
      --checkpoint-remote string        Checkpoint remote in provider:owner/repo format (e.g., github:org/checkpoints-repo)
  -f, --force                           Reinstall the Entire git hook
  -h, --help                            help for configure
      --local                           Write settings to .entire/settings.local.json instead of .entire/settings.json
      --project                         Write settings to .entire/settings.json even if it already exists
      --skip-push-sessions              Disable automatic pushing of session logs on git push
      --summarize-model string          Set the model hint used by explain --generate

      --summarize-timeout-seconds int   Set the hard deadline (seconds) for explain --generate summary generation. 0 clears (falls back to 5m default).
      --telemetry                       Enable anonymous usage analytics (default true)
```

> AGENT

Yep. I looked at the local `entire` CLI, and the tighter parallel is:

**Planetfall should not be “find artifact → create checkpoint.”**  
It should be **“receive task → make repair → commit repair → checkpoint captures the repair session.”**

What Entire actually models:

1. Agent/session starts with an intent prompt.
2. Work happens in the repo.
3. A git commit is made.
4. Entire hooks attach an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer and condense the session.
5. `entire checkpoint explain` can later show: checkpoint id, session id, author, tokens, linked commit, intent prompt, files touched, and transcript/summary.
6. `rewind`, `recap`, `activity`, `dispatch`, and `search` are ways to navigate that saved work history.

So in the game, I’d make every artifact a tiny “coding with Entire” loop:

| Entire | Planetfall Parallel |
|---|---|
| User prompt / issue | Ship AI gives you a broken subsystem task |
| Code change | Player repairs a small file/config/log in an in-game terminal |
| Tests/build pass | Ship subsystem stabilizes |
| Git commit | Player commits the repair with a message |
| Entire checkpoint | Memory shard is sealed to that commit |
| Transcript | Ship recovers the conversation/log of how it was fixed |
| `checkpoint explain` | Inspect why that repair exists |
| `recap` | Summarize recovered memories so far |
| `activity` | Timeline of crash/repair work |
| `rewind` | Roll back a bad repair/checkpoint |
| `dispatch` | Final rescue report assembled from all restored checkpoints |

The big design change: the **checkpoint button should move after the repair/commit**, not right after inspection. Inspecting an artifact should open a task, not complete it.

A really good MVP arc would be:

1. **Helmet / `recap`**
   The AI wakes up confused. Player runs/uses recap to understand the recent mission context.

2. **Logbook / `checkpoint`**
   Player repairs a small corrupted file, commits it, then creates/locks the checkpoint. This teaches that checkpoints are tied to commits.

3. **Nav Panel / `activity`**
   Player reads the activity timeline to identify when the crash/anomaly started.

4. **Beacon / `dispatch`**
   Player generates the final dispatch from the collected checkpoints/transcripts. That is the win condition.

The current uncommitted checkpoint UI is a good seed, but I’d reframe its copy and flow. Instead of “You just recovered a lost memory. Create a checkpoint,” it should become something like: “Repair committed. Link this commit to a checkpoint?” Then the panel can show a fake checkpoint id, commit hash, intent, files touched, and transcript snippet. That will make it feel *much* closer to Entire.

> DEVELOPER

okay we dont need to do like activity and dispatch and all that..this is just the first level but im trying to say..in the first level..i want to follow the real flow..but it will just like teaching people how to create checkpoitns or how checkpoints are created. i just wnat it to be parallel

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. For level one, make the whole lesson:

**A checkpoint is not the thing you click. A checkpoint is what Entire creates around committed work.**

So the first level should follow this loop:

1. **Ship gives you a task**
   “Repair the helmet logger so the AI can remember the last EVA sequence.”

2. **You make a small code/config change**
   The player patches one obvious broken line in a tiny in-game file.

3. **You review the diff**
   Show a simple `git diff` style panel.

4. **You commit the change**
   The player presses something like `Commit repair`, maybe with a commit message.

5. **Entire hook runs**
   The UI shows:
   `prepare-commit-msg`
   `commit-msg`
   `post-commit`
   Then: `Entire-Checkpoint: ab12cd34ef56`

6. **Checkpoint record appears**
   The recovered memory is now tied to:
   - commit hash
   - commit message
   - checkpoint id
   - original task prompt
   - transcript snippet
   - files touched

7. **Then the memory is restored**
   Not because you found the helmet, but because you completed the work and committed it.

So I’d change the current first-level interaction from:

`Inspect artifact → Create checkpoint → memory restored`

to:

`Inspect artifact → receive repair task → edit/fix → commit → Entire creates checkpoint → memory restored`

The button should probably not say **Create checkpoint** at first. Better:

- Before work: `Begin repair`
- After edit: `Review diff`
- After review: `Commit repair`
- After commit: passive status says `Checkpoint created`

That’s the parallel. The player learns: “I don’t checkpoint random vibes. I do work, commit it, and Entire preserves the context around that work.”

> DEVELOPER

how are they going to do repairs..why repairs..remember their spaceship crashed

> AGENT

Right, good catch. “Repairs” might be the wrong word if it sounds like fixing the spaceship hull.

For level one, I’d frame it as **memory reconstruction**, not mechanical repair.

The ship crashed because the AI lost context. So the player is not fixing engines yet. They’re helping the AI rebuild a trustworthy memory record from scattered evidence.

The parallel to coding with Entire becomes:

1. **Crash scattered the context**
   The helmet contains a damaged EVA logger: partial transcript, broken timestamp, maybe one missing line.

2. **Player recovers evidence**
   They inspect the helmet and get a small “work item”:
   `Restore helmet logger context`

3. **Player makes a tiny change**
   Not “repair the helmet,” but update the ship’s memory file:
   `memory/eva-logger.txt`
   or `ship-context.json`

   Example: choose the correct missing line from clues on the artifact.

4. **Player commits the restored memory**
   The AI says: “This memory is unstable until it is committed.”

5. **Entire creates a checkpoint**
   The checkpoint preserves:
   - what changed
   - why it changed
   - the commit
   - the player/AI transcript around the reconstruction

6. **The AI remembers**
   The restored memory becomes part of the ship’s stable context.

So the task is not:

`fix spaceship part → checkpoint`

It is:

`recover lost context → update memory record → commit → checkpoint captures the work`

That feels much more faithful to Entire and to the crash story.

I’d probably avoid the word **repair** in the UI. Use words like:

- `Recover`
- `Reconstruct`
- `Restore memory`
- `Commit restored context`
- `Checkpoint created`

The helmet is just the first physical clue. The actual “coding” action is restoring the ship AI’s memory repo.

> DEVELOPER

ok so what does that literally look like in the game

> AGENT

Literally, level one could look like this:

You land on the island and find the **Abandoned EVA Helmet** half-buried in the gold terrain. When you press `E`, the game does not say “checkpoint this item.” It opens a small ship-memory console.

**Screen 1: Artifact**
The helmet panel says:

> EVA helmet logger found.  
> The ship AI has loose evidence, but no stable memory. Reconstruct the missing context, commit it, and Entire will preserve the work as a checkpoint.

Button: `Reconstruct memory`

**Screen 2: Tiny File Editor**
A simple in-game editor opens, like a mini repo:

```txt
ship-memory/eva-03.log

Mission: Survey landing island
Status: interrupted
Last stable memory:
[MISSING]
```

Next to it, the helmet transcript fragment shows clues:

```txt
...gold ridge visible...
...marked landing site...
...signal loss in 00:14...
```

The player chooses or drags the correct recovered line into the missing slot:

```txt
Last stable memory:
Marked the gold island as the landing site before signal loss.
```

So the “code change” is really a **memory-file change**. They are editing the ship AI’s context repo.

**Screen 3: Diff**
Then the game shows a fake-but-real-feeling diff:

```diff
diff --git a/ship-memory/eva-03.log b/ship-memory/eva-03.log
- [MISSING]
+ Marked the gold island as the landing site before signal loss.
```

Button: `Commit restored memory`

**Screen 4: Commit**
The player sees a commit message:

```txt
Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory
```

They press `Commit`.

Then the terminal-style output appears:

```txt
git commit -m "Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory"

[main a17c9e2] Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory
 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)

entire hooks git post-commit
Entire-Checkpoint: 9f42c8a1d6b3
```

**Screen 5: Checkpoint Record**
Now the game shows the checkpoint as the reward:

```txt
Checkpoint created

Commit: a17c9e2
Message: Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory
Checkpoint: 9f42c8a1d6b3
Session intent: Recover context from the EVA helmet
Files touched: ship-memory/eva-03.log
Transcript saved: yes
```

Then the ship AI says something like:

> This memory is stable now. I can remember why we landed here.

That is the parallel.

The player did not “checkpoint an artifact.” They found evidence, changed a memory file, committed the change, and then Entire created a checkpoint tied to that commit and the context of the work.

The physical gameplay is still simple: walk to helmet, inspect it, solve a tiny reconstruction puzzle, commit it, watch the checkpoint form. The conceptual lesson is very real: **checkpoints are born from committed work plus the transcript/context around that work.**

> DEVELOPER

will the user know what to do? like is it too easy or too hard for the user

> AGENT

It’ll be too hard if we make the player infer the Entire model from raw terms like “commit,” “checkpoint,” and “transcript.”

It’ll be too easy in a bad way if it’s just `Click helmet → click checkpoint`.

So level one should be **guided, but not fake**. The player should always know the next action, while still experiencing the real sequence.

I’d make it a tutorialized “do the thing” flow:

1. The helmet glows.
   Prompt: `Press E to inspect`

2. Panel opens.
   AI says: “This helmet has evidence, but I cannot trust it until we save the recovered context.”

3. Button: `Open memory file`
   Not “solve puzzle” yet. Just clear next step.

4. File editor appears with one obvious missing line.
   The clue is beside it. The player chooses from 3 possible lines, not free-types.

5. After they choose, the diff appears automatically.
   AI says: “You changed the ship memory. Now commit the change.”

6. Button: `Commit restored memory`
   The user does not need to know git yet. The game shows the command/output after the button.

7. Checkpoint animation/output appears.
   AI says: “Entire tied the commit to this work session. That is a checkpoint.”

That means the first level’s challenge is not “can you figure out what to do?” The challenge is “can you understand what just happened?”

To keep it balanced:

- **Easy action:** choose one missing memory line.
- **Real concept:** change → diff → commit → checkpoint.
- **No typing required** in level one.
- **Terminal output visible**, but not required to operate.
- **AI narration explains after each action**, not in a giant intro.
- **Checkpoint is the reward**, not the button.

The player should leave level one knowing one sentence:

> “I made a change, committed it, and Entire saved the context around that commit as a checkpoint.”

That’s the win.

> DEVELOPER

what would make it a game..like how do they win or lose or something

> AGENT

For level one, I would make it a **soft-fail puzzle**, not a death game.

The game is:

**Can you recover the correct memory, commit it, and create a valid checkpoint before the AI’s context buffer collapses?**

Literal win condition:

1. Inspect helmet.
2. Choose the correct missing memory line from evidence.
3. Review the diff.
4. Commit it with a good commit message.
5. Entire checkpoint appears.
6. The AI remembers the landing sequence.
7. A sealed path/door/signal on the island unlocks.

Literal lose/fail condition:

- If they pick the wrong memory line, the ship runs a “memory consistency check” and it fails.
- The AI says the reconstructed context contradicts the evidence.
- The player can try again, but the **context stability meter** drops.
- If stability hits zero, the AI forgets the clue layout and the puzzle resets.

So it has stakes, but it is not punishing.

Example:

```txt
CONTEXT STABILITY: 3/3
```

Wrong reconstruction:

```txt
memory check failed
expected: landing site context
found: engine-room context

CONTEXT STABILITY: 2/3
```

Wrong commit message could be a smaller mistake:

```txt
Commit message too vague.
A checkpoint is useful when future-you can tell what changed.
Try a message that names the recovered memory.
```

Win output:

```txt
[main a17c9e2] Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory
 1 file changed

Entire-Checkpoint: 9f42c8a1d6b3
Transcript saved.
Memory stabilized.
```

Then the island reacts: helmet beam shuts off, AI voice becomes clearer, and maybe a small bridge/door opens to end the level.

That makes it a game because there is:

- a clear objective
- a puzzle
- wrong answers
- feedback
- limited tries or a stability meter
- a concrete reward
- a level-complete moment

And it still teaches the real flow: **change → validate → commit → checkpoint → future context recovered.**

> DEVELOPER

so there's like a time limit or something?

> AGENT

Not a real-time timer, I think. A timer would make it feel like an arcade pressure puzzle, and that is not very parallel to coding with Entire.

Better: use a **context stability meter** or **attempt budget**.

So instead of:

```txt
02:00 remaining
```

Use:

```txt
CONTEXT STABILITY: 3/3
```

Each wrong reconstruction lowers stability:

```txt
Memory check failed.
The recovered line contradicts the helmet transcript.

CONTEXT STABILITY: 2/3
```

If it reaches zero, the AI loses the thread:

```txt
Context buffer collapsed.
Restoring last stable state...
```

Then the puzzle resets to before the memory edit.

That maps better to the theme: the danger is not “you ran out of time,” it is **the AI cannot hold unstable context forever**.

For level one, I’d make it forgiving:

- 3 stability points.
- Wrong memory line costs 1.
- Bad commit message does not cost a point; it just asks you to improve it.
- Reviewing the diff is mandatory, but not difficult.
- Losing resets only the little console puzzle, not the whole island.

So the game loop is:

`inspect helmet → reconstruct memory → check consistency → commit → checkpoint`

The “lose” condition is failing to stabilize the memory before the context buffer collapses. That gives stakes without making the tutorial stressful.

> DEVELOPER

okay try implementing this..lets see..i would make it as graphic heavy as we possibly can i think..what do you think

> AGENT

I like “graphic heavy” for this, with one caveat: the visuals should dramatize the real workflow, not bury it. I’m going to make level one feel like a ship-memory reconstruction station: evidence, file edit, diff, commit output, hook/checkpoint formation, and a visible context stability meter.

```
---
name: frontend-design
description: Create distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces with high design quality. Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, artifacts, posters, or applications (examples include websites, landing pages, dashboards, React components, HTML/CSS layouts, or when styling/beautifying any web UI). Generates creative, polished code and UI design that avoids generic AI aesthetics.
license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt
---

This skill guides creation of distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces that avoid generic "AI slop" aesthetics. Implement real working code with exceptional attention to aesthetic details and creative choices.

The user provides frontend requirements: a component, page, application, or interface to build. They may include context about the purpose, audience, or technical constraints.

## Design Thinking

Before coding, understand the context and commit to a BOLD aesthetic direction:
- **Purpose**: What problem does this interface solve? Who uses it?
- **Tone**: Pick an extreme: brutally minimal, maximalist chaos, retro-futuristic, organic/natural, luxury/refined, playful/toy-like, editorial/magazine, brutalist/raw, art deco/geometric, soft/pastel, industrial/utilitarian, etc. There are so many flavors to choose from. Use these for inspiration but design one that is true to the aesthetic direction.
- **Constraints**: Technical requirements (framework, performance, accessibility).
- **Differentiation**: What makes this UNFORGETTABLE? What's the one thing someone will remember?

**CRITICAL**: Choose a clear conceptual direction and execute it with precision. Bold maximalism and refined minimalism both work - the key is intentionality, not intensity.

Then implement working code (HTML/CSS/JS, React, Vue, etc.) that is:
- Production-grade and functional
- Visually striking and memorable
- Cohesive with a clear aesthetic point-of-view
- Meticulously refined in every detail

## Frontend Aesthetics Guidelines

Focus on:
- **Typography**: Choose fonts that are beautiful, unique, and interesting. Avoid generic fonts like Arial and Inter; opt instead for distinctive choices that elevate the frontend's aesthetics; unexpected, characterful font choices. Pair a distinctive display font with a refined body font.
- **Color & Theme**: Commit to a cohesive aesthetic. Use CSS variables for consistency. Dominant colors with sharp accents outperform timid, evenly-distributed palettes.
- **Motion**: Use animations for effects and micro-interactions. Prioritize CSS-only solutions for HTML. Use Motion library for React when available. Focus on high-impact moments: one well-orchestrated page load with staggered reveals (animation-delay) creates more delight than scattered micro-interactions. Use scroll-triggering and hover states that surprise.
- **Spatial Composition**: Unexpected layouts. Asymmetry. Overlap. Diagonal flow. Grid-breaking elements. Generous negative space OR controlled density.
- **Backgrounds & Visual Details**: Create atmosphere and depth rather than defaulting to solid colors. Add contextual effects and textures that match the overall aesthetic. Apply creative forms like gradient meshes, noise textures, geometric patterns, layered transparencies, dramatic shadows, decorative borders, custom cursors, and grain overlays.

NEVER use generic AI-generated aesthetics like overused font families (Inter, Roboto, Arial, system fonts), cliched color schemes (particularly purple gradients on white backgrounds), predictable layouts and component patterns, and cookie-cutter design that lacks context-specific character.

Interpret creatively and make unexpected choices that feel genuinely designed for the context. No design should be the same. Vary between light and dark themes, different fonts, different aesthetics. NEVER converge on common choices (Space Grotesk, for example) across generations.

**IMPORTANT**: Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision. Maximalist designs need elaborate code with extensive animations and effects. Minimalist or refined designs need restraint, precision, and careful attention to spacing, typography, and subtle details. Elegance comes from executing the vision well.

Remember: Claude is capable of extraordinary creative work. Don't hold back, show what can truly be created when thinking outside the box and committing fully to a distinctive vision.
```

```
* { margin: 0; padding: 0; box-sizing: border-box; }

/* Background music controls */
#audio-panel {
  position: absolute;
  top: 28px;
  right: 32px;
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 8px;
  padding: 8px 10px;
  background: rgba(8, 14, 26, 0.58);
  border: 1px solid rgba(255, 244, 232, 0.18);
  border-radius: 6px;
  box-shadow: 0 12px 36px rgba(2, 4, 10, 0.34);
  backdrop-filter: blur(10px);
  pointer-events: auto;
}

.audio-mark {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 30px;
  height: 30px;
  color: rgba(255, 210, 122, 0.84);
}

.icon-button {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  width: 30px;
  height: 30px;
  border: 1px solid rgba(255, 244, 232, 0.22);
  border-radius: 50%;
  background: rgba(255, 244, 232, 0.08);
  color: var(--ink);
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background 0.18s ease, border-color 0.18s ease, transform 0.18s ease;
}

.icon-button:hover,
.icon-button:focus-visible {
  background: rgba(255, 184, 107, 0.16);
  border-color: rgba(255, 210, 122, 0.55);
  outline: none;
  transform: translateY(-1px);
}

#audio-panel.is-awaiting-start #audio-toggle {
  border-color: rgba(255, 210, 122, 0.7);

}

.icon {
  width: 15px;
  height: 15px;
  display: block;
  fill: currentColor;
}

.icon-pause,
#audio-toggle.is-playing .icon-play {
  display: none;
}

#audio-toggle.is-playing .icon-pause {
  display: block;
}

.icon-speaker .mute-mark {
  fill: none;
  stroke: currentColor;
  stroke-width: 1.8;
  stroke-linecap: round;
  opacity: 0;
}

#audio-mute.is-muted .mute-mark {
  opacity: 1;
}

#audio-volume {
  width: 92px;
  accent-color: var(--accent-warm);
  cursor: pointer;
}

#audio-volume:focus-visible {
  outline: 1px solid rgba(255, 210, 122, 0.75);
  outline-offset: 3px;
}

@keyframes audio-start-pulse {

}

/* Inspection panel */
#panel {

}

#panel.hidden {

#panel-tag {

}

#panel h2 {

#panel p {

#panel-foot {

/* Checkpoint offer inside the inspection panel */
#panel-checkpoint {
  margin-top: 26px;
  padding-top: 22px;
  border-top: 1px solid rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.18);
}

.ckpt-q {
  font-size: 13px;

}

.ckpt-explainer {
  font-size: 13px;
  line-height: 1.65;
  opacity: 0.78;

}

.ckpt-explainer strong {
  color: var(--ink);
  opacity: 1;
  font-weight: 600;
}

#ckpt-create {
  width: 100%;
  padding: 13px 16px;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 12px;
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: #04121c;
  background: var(--accent);
  border: none;
  border-radius: 8px;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: transform 0.15s ease, box-shadow 0.2s ease;
  box-shadow: 0 6px 22px rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.28);
}

#ckpt-create:hover {
  transform: translateY(-1px);
  box-shadow: 0 10px 30px rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.42);
}

#ckpt-create:active { transform: translateY(0); }

/* :not(.hidden) so the ID rule doesn't out-specificity .hidden's display:none */
#ckpt-done:not(.hidden) {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 14px;
}

.ckpt-check {
  flex: none;
  width: 34px;
  height: 34px;
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  border-radius: 50%;
  background: rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.16);
  border: 1px solid var(--accent);
  color: var(--accent);
  font-size: 16px;
}

.ckpt-done-title { font-size: 14px; letter-spacing: 0.04em; }
.ckpt-done-sub { font-size: 12px; opacity: 0.6; margin-top: 3px; }

#panel-close {
  position: absolute;
  top: 26px;
  right: 28px;
  width: 38px;
  height: 38px;
  border: 1px solid rgba(232, 244, 255, 0.25);
  background: transparent;
  color: var(--ink);
  font-size: 20px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background 0.2s, border-color 0.2s;
}

#panel-close:hover {
  background: rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.12);
  border-color: var(--accent);
}

/* Map pin marking the landable island */
#island-pin {
  position: fixed;
  top: 0; left: 0;
  transform: translate(-50%, -100%);
  padding: 0;
  background: none;
  border: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  pointer-events: auto;
  z-index: 15;
  transition: filter 0.2s ease, transform 0.18s cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
  transform-origin: bottom center;
  filter:

}
#island-pin .pin-svg { display: block; }
#island-pin .pin-svg path {
  fill: var(--pin-shell);
  stroke: var(--pin-rim);
  stroke-width: 1.35;
}
#island-pin .pin-svg circle {
  fill: var(--pin-core);
}
/* Hover (or keyboard focus, or synced from the 3D region) lifts the pin. */
#island-pin:hover,
#island-pin:focus-visible,
#island-pin.is-hot {
  transform: translate(-50%, -100%) scale(1.12);
  filter:
    drop-shadow(0 3px 5px var(--pin-shadow))
    drop-shadow(0 0 12px rgba(255, 184, 107, 0.62));

}

/* Crossfade overlay between views */
#fade {
```

```
.arrow-keys .key {

  width: 18px;
  height: 18px;
  padding: 0;
  font-size: 9px;
}

.key-up { grid-column: 2; grid-row: 1; }
.key-left { grid-column: 1; grid-row: 2; }
.key-down { grid-column: 2; grid-row: 2; }
.key-right { grid-column: 3; grid-row: 2; }

.mouse-hint {
  display: inline-flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 6px;
  color: rgba(232, 244, 255, 0.82);
}

.mouse-icon {
  position: relative;
  width: 13px;
  height: 19px;
  border: 1px solid rgba(255, 244, 232, 0.5);
  border-radius: 8px;
  background: rgba(255, 244, 232, 0.08);
}

.mouse-icon::before {
  content: "";
  position: absolute;
  top: 4px;
  left: 50%;
  width: 2px;
  height: 5px;
  transform: translateX(-50%);
  border-radius: 2px;
  background: rgba(255, 210, 122, 0.72);
}

@media (max-width: 680px) {
  #hint {
    bottom: 72px;
    max-width: calc(100vw - 36px);
    text-align: center;
    white-space: normal;
  }

  #audio-panel {
    top: auto;
    right: auto;
    bottom: 22px;
    left: 18px;
    gap: 6px;
    padding: 7px 8px;
  }

  .audio-mark {
    width: 28px;
    height: 28px;
  }

  .icon-button {
    width: 28px;
    height: 28px;
  }

  #audio-volume {
    width: 78px;
  }

  #fp-controls {
    top: 116px;
    right: 18px;
    bottom: auto;
    max-width: calc(100vw - 36px);
  }

  #fp-prompt {
    bottom: 82px;
  }
}

/* Loader */
#loader {
  position: fixed;
  inset: 0;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: center;
  gap: 22px;
  background: #02040a;
  z-index: 50;
  transition: opacity 0.8s ease;
}

#loader.done {
  opacity: 0;
  pointer-events: none;
}

.loader-ring {
  width: 46px;
  height: 46px;
  border: 2px solid rgba(111, 227, 255, 0.2);
  border-top-color: var(--accent);
  border-radius: 50%;
  animation: spin 0.9s linear infinite;
}

.loader-text {
  font-size: 12px;
  letter-spacing: 0.35em;
  opacity: 0.7;
}

@keyframes spin { to { transform: rotate(360deg); } }

.hidden { display: none; }
```

```
import * as THREE from "three";
import { createPlanetView } from "./planetView.js";
import { createIslandView } from "./islandView.js";

const canvas = document.getElementById("scene");
const renderer = new THREE.WebGLRenderer({ canvas, antialias: true });
renderer.setSize(window.innerWidth, window.innerHeight);
renderer.setPixelRatio(Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio, 2));
renderer.toneMapping = THREE.ACESFilmicToneMapping;
renderer.toneMappingExposure = 0.92;
renderer.outputColorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;

// ---------- shared inspection panel ----------
const panel = document.getElementById("panel");
const panelTitle = document.getElementById("panel-title");
const panelBody = document.getElementById("panel-body");
const panelFoot = document.getElementById("panel-foot");
const ckptOffer = document.getElementById("ckpt-offer");
const ckptDone = document.getElementById("ckpt-done");
const ckptCreate = document.getElementById("ckpt-create");

// The artifact context for the panel that's currently open. islandView hands us
// the fragment plus how to mark it checkpointed and how to read overall progress.
let panelCtx = null;

function renderFoot(progress) {
  if (!progress) { panelFoot.textContent = "memory fragment"; return; }
  const { recovered, total } = progress;
  panelFoot.textContent = recovered >= total
    ? "all core memories restored · the ship remembers itself"
    : `core memories restored · ${recovered}/${total}`;
}

function showCheckpointState(saved) {
  ckptOffer.classList.toggle("hidden", saved);
  ckptDone.classList.toggle("hidden", !saved);
}

function openPanel(frag, ctx = {}) {
  panelCtx = ctx;
  panelTitle.textContent = frag.title;
  panelBody.textContent = frag.body;
  showCheckpointState(!!ctx.checkpointed);
  renderFoot(ctx.progress);
  panel.classList.remove("hidden");
}
function closePanel() { panel.classList.add("hidden"); }

// Create a checkpoint for the artifact in the open panel: lock the memory in,
// then flip the panel to its "saved" state and update the progress counter.
ckptCreate?.addEventListener("click", () => {
  if (!panelCtx?.onCreate) return;
  const progress = panelCtx.onCreate();
  panelCtx.checkpointed = true;
  showCheckpointState(true);
  renderFoot(progress);
});
document.getElementById("panel-close").addEventListener("click", closePanel);
window.addEventListener("keydown", (e) => { if (e.key === "Escape") closePanel(); });

// ---------- background music ----------
const bgm = document.getElementById("bgm");
const audioPanel = document.getElementById("audio-panel");
const audioToggle = document.getElementById("audio-toggle");
const audioMute = document.getElementById("audio-mute");
const audioVolume = document.getElementById("audio-volume");
let userPausedMusic = false;

if (bgm && audioPanel && audioToggle && audioMute && audioVolume) {
  bgm.volume = Number(audioVolume.value);
  bgm.autoplay = true;
  bgm.loop = true;

  const updateAudioUi = () => {
    const playing = !bgm.paused;
    const awaitingStart = !playing && !userPausedMusic;
    audioPanel.classList.toggle("is-awaiting-start", awaitingStart);
    audioToggle.classList.toggle("is-playing", playing);
    audioToggle.setAttribute("aria-label", playing ? "Pause music" : "Start music");
    audioToggle.title = playing ? "Pause music" : "Start music";
    audioMute.classList.toggle("is-muted", bgm.muted || bgm.volume === 0);
    audioMute.setAttribute("aria-label", bgm.muted ? "Unmute music" : "Mute music");
    audioMute.title = bgm.muted ? "Unmute music" : "Mute music";
  };

  const tryPlayMusic = () => {
    if (userPausedMusic) return;
    bgm.play().then(updateAudioUi).catch(updateAudioUi);
  };
  const removeGestureMusicStart = () => {
    for (const eventName of ["pointerdown", "click", "keydown", "touchstart"]) {
      window.removeEventListener(eventName, startMusicOnGesture, true);
    }
  };
  const startMusicOnGesture = (event) => {
    if (event.target?.closest?.("#audio-panel")) return;
    tryPlayMusic();
  };

  audioToggle.addEventListener("click", () => {
    if (bgm.paused) {
      userPausedMusic = false;
      tryPlayMusic();
    } else {
      userPausedMusic = true;
      bgm.pause();
      updateAudioUi();
    }
  });

  audioMute.addEventListener("click", () => {
    bgm.muted = !bgm.muted;
    updateAudioUi();
  });

  audioVolume.addEventListener("input", () => {
    bgm.volume = Number(audioVolume.value);
    if (bgm.volume > 0 && bgm.muted) bgm.muted = false;
    updateAudioUi();
  });

  bgm.addEventListener("play", () => {
    removeGestureMusicStart();
    updateAudioUi();
  });
  bgm.addEventListener("pause", updateAudioUi);
  bgm.addEventListener("volumechange", updateAudioUi);

  for (const eventName of ["pointerdown", "click", "keydown", "touchstart"]) {
    window.addEventListener(eventName, startMusicOnGesture, true);
  }

  tryPlayMusic();
  updateAudioUi();
}

// ---------- views ----------
const planetView = createPlanetView(renderer, {
  onIslandClick: () => switchTo(islandView),
});
const islandView = createIslandView(renderer, {
  openPanel,
  onExit: () => { closePanel(); switchTo(planetView); },
});

// Default to the orbit view; ?view=island jumps straight onto the island
// (handy for development — pointer-lock still needs a real click).
let current = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get("view") === "island"
  ? islandView : planetView;
current.enter();

// ---------- fade transition ----------
const fade = document.getElementById("fade");
const hint = document.getElementById("hint");
let transitioning = false;
hint.classList.toggle("hidden", current !== planetView);

function switchTo(view) {
  if (transitioning || view === current) return;
  transitioning = true;
  fade.classList.add("show");
  setTimeout(() => {
    current.exit();
    current = view;
    current.enter();
    hint.classList.toggle("hidden", current !== planetView);
    fade.classList.remove("show");
    transitioning = false;
  }, 650); // match #fade CSS transition
}

// ---------- resize ----------
window.addEventListener("resize", () => {
  renderer.setSize(window.innerWidth, window.innerHeight);
  planetView.resize();
  islandView.resize();
});

// ---------- loop ----------
const clock = new THREE.Clock();
let firstFrame = true;

function animate() {
  const dt = Math.min(clock.getDelta(), 0.05); // clamp big tab-switch jumps
  const t = clock.elapsedTime;
  current.update(dt, t);
  renderer.render(current.scene, current.camera);

  if (firstFrame) {
    firstFrame = false;
    document.getElementById("loader").classList.add("done");
  }
  requestAnimationFrame(animate);
}
animate();
```

```
import * as THREE from "three";
import { createTerrain } from "./terrain.js";
import { createFirstPerson } from "./firstPerson.js";
import { FRAGMENTS, BUILDERS } from "./debris.js";

// The walkable island level. First-person: you wander the terrain and walk up
// to the scattered artifacts to inspect them. One island, several artifacts —
// each seeds a context-recovery puzzle.

const INTERACT_DIST = 7;   // how close (world units, XZ) to inspect an artifact
const BEAM_COLOR = 0xffd27a; // warm gold to match the planet's islands

// Where each fragment sits on the island (world XZ), spread around the centre.
const PLACEMENTS = [
  { x: -34, z: -28 },
  { x: 40, z: -18 },
  { x: 28, z: 38 },
  { x: -30, z: 36 },
];

function makeBeamTexture() {
  const c = document.createElement("canvas");
  c.width = 8; c.height = 128;
  const ctx = c.getContext("2d");
  const g = ctx.createLinearGradient(0, 0, 0, 128);
  g.addColorStop(0, "rgba(255,210,122,0)");
  g.addColorStop(1, "rgba(255,210,122,0.9)");
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, 8, 128);
  const t = new THREE.CanvasTexture(c);
  t.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
  return t;
}

export function createIslandView(renderer, { openPanel, onExit } = {}) {
  const canvas = renderer.domElement;

  // ---------- scene & sky ----------
  const scene = new THREE.Scene();
  scene.background = new THREE.Color(0x2a2350); // lavender dusk sky
  scene.fog = new THREE.Fog(0x2a2350, 90, 320);

  const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(
    62, window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight, 0.1, 2000
  );

  // ---------- lighting (soft, warm sun in a lavender sky) ----------
  const hemi = new THREE.HemisphereLight(0xcdbcff, 0x3a2f5e, 0.7);
  scene.add(hemi);
  const sun = new THREE.DirectionalLight(0xfff1dc, 1.5);
  sun.position.set(60, 90, 40);
  scene.add(sun);
  scene.add(new THREE.AmbientLight(0x6a5a92, 0.3));

  // Big sky dome so the horizon isn't an empty void.
  const dome = new THREE.Mesh(
    new THREE.SphereGeometry(900, 32, 16),
    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: 0x3a3168, side: THREE.BackSide, fog: false })
  );
  scene.add(dome);

  // ---------- terrain ----------
  const terrain = createTerrain({ size: 200, segments: 220, maxHeight: 26, seed: 1337 });
  scene.add(terrain.mesh, terrain.water);

  // ---------- artifacts ----------
  const beamTex = makeBeamTexture();
  const artifacts = [];
  FRAGMENTS.forEach((frag, idx) => {
    const place = PLACEMENTS[idx % PLACEMENTS.length];
    const groundY = terrain.heightAt(place.x, place.z);
    const anchor = new THREE.Group();
    anchor.position.set(place.x, groundY, place.z);

    const model = BUILDERS[frag.kind]();
    model.scale.setScalar(14); // the props are planet-scale tiny; size up for FP
    model.position.y = 1.4;
    anchor.add(model);

    // A tall light beam so the artifact is findable from across the island.
    const beam = new THREE.Mesh(
      new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.6, 0.6, 60, 12, 1, true),
      new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({
        map: beamTex, color: BEAM_COLOR, transparent: true, opacity: 0.5,
        blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending, depthWrite: false, side: THREE.DoubleSide,
        fog: false,
      })
    );
    beam.position.y = 30;
    anchor.add(beam);

    const glow = new THREE.PointLight(BEAM_COLOR, 8, 40, 2);
    glow.position.y = 3;
    anchor.add(glow);

    scene.add(anchor);
    artifacts.push({ anchor, model, beam, fragment: frag, collected: false });
  });

  // ---------- first-person controller ----------
  const fp = createFirstPerson(camera, canvas, {
    heightAt: terrain.heightAt,
    radius: terrain.radius * 0.96,
    eyeHeight: 2.6,
    speed: 24,
  });
  scene.add(fp.controls.object);

  // ---------- HUD elements ----------
  const promptEl = document.getElementById("fp-prompt");
  const controlsEl = document.getElementById("fp-controls");
  const crosshair = document.getElementById("crosshair");
  const islandHud = document.getElementById("island-hud");

  let target = null;     // nearest artifact in range
  let active = false;    // is this view the one being shown?
  let promptText = null;
  let controlsLocked = null;

  // How many core memories have been checkpointed (restored), out of the total.
  const progressNow = () => ({
    recovered: artifacts.filter((a) => a.collected).length,
    total: artifacts.length,
  });

  function setPrompt(text) {
    if (!promptEl) return;
    if (text === promptText) return;
    promptText = text;
    if (text) { promptEl.textContent = text; promptEl.classList.remove("hidden"); }
    else promptEl.classList.add("hidden");
  }
  function setControls(locked) {
    if (!controlsEl) return;
    if (locked === controlsLocked && !controlsEl.classList.contains("hidden")) return;
    controlsLocked = locked;
    controlsEl.innerHTML = `
      <span class="control-item">
        <span class="control-label">To move</span>
        <span class="arrow-keys" aria-label="Arrow keys">
          <span class="key key-up">↑</span>
          <span class="key key-left">←</span>
          <span class="key key-down">↓</span>
          <span class="key key-right">→</span>
        </span>
      </span>
      ${locked ? `
        <span class="control-item">
          <span class="control-label">To look around</span>
          <span class="mouse-hint">
            <span class="mouse-icon" aria-hidden="true"></span>
            <span>move mouse</span>
          </span>
        </span>
        <span class="control-item">
          <span class="control-label">Free cursor</span>
          <span class="key key-wide">Esc</span>
        </span>
      ` : ""}
      <span class="control-item">
        <span class="control-label">Return to orbit</span>
        <span class="key">B</span>
      </span>
    `;
    controlsEl.classList.remove("hidden");
  }
  function hideControls() {
    controlsLocked = null;
    controlsEl?.classList.add("hidden");
  }

  // ---------- input ----------
  function onCanvasClick() {
    if (active && !fp.isLocked) fp.lock();
  }
  function onKeyDown(e) {
    if (!active) return;
    if (e.code === "Escape" && fp.isLocked) fp.unlock();
    if (e.code === "KeyE" && target) {
      fp.unlock();              // free the mouse for the panel
      const a = target;         // inspecting offers a checkpoint; it isn't saved
      openPanel?.(a.fragment, { //   until the player chooses to create one.
        checkpointed: a.collected,
        progress: progressNow(),
        onCreate: () => { a.collected = true; return progressNow(); },
      });
    }
    if (e.code === "KeyB") onExit?.(); // leave the island, back to orbit
  }
  // Show the crosshair only while the mouse is captured.
  fp.controls.addEventListener("lock", () => crosshair?.classList.remove("hidden"));
  fp.controls.addEventListener("unlock", () => crosshair?.classList.add("hidden"));

  // ---------- per-frame ----------
  const clockOffset = Math.random() * 10;
  function update(dt, t) {
    fp.update(dt);

    // Find the nearest artifact within reach (ignoring height).
    let nearest = null, nearestD = Infinity;
    for (const a of artifacts) {
      const dx = camera.position.x - a.anchor.position.x;
      const dz = camera.position.z - a.anchor.position.z;
      const d = Math.hypot(dx, dz);
      if (d < nearestD) { nearestD = d; nearest = a; }
      // Idle spin + bob on the prop.
      a.model.rotation.y += dt * 0.6;
      a.model.position.y = 1.4 + Math.sin((t + clockOffset) * 1.5 + a.anchor.position.x) * 0.4;
      a.beam.material.opacity = a.collected ? 0.12 : 0.4 + Math.sin(t * 2) * 0.12;
    }
    target = nearestD <= INTERACT_DIST ? nearest : null;

    if (active) {
      setControls(fp.isLocked);
      if (!fp.isLocked) setPrompt("Click to look around");
      else if (target) {
        setPrompt(`Press E to inspect · ${target.fragment.title}` +
          (target.collected ? "  · ✓ checkpointed" : ""));
      } else setPrompt("Find the glowing artifacts");
    }
  }

  // ---------- lifecycle ----------
  function enter() {
    active = true;
    fp.attach();
    // Drop the player near the south shore, looking toward the centre.
    fp.groundAt(0, terrain.radius * 0.75);
    camera.lookAt(0, terrain.heightAt(0, 0) + 6, 0);
    canvas.addEventListener("click", onCanvasClick);
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
    islandHud?.classList.remove("hidden");
    setControls(false);
    crosshair?.classList.add("hidden");
  }
  function exit() {
    active = false;
    fp.unlock();
    fp.detach();
    canvas.removeEventListener("click", onCanvasClick);
    window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
    setPrompt(null);
    hideControls();
    islandHud?.classList.add("hidden");
    crosshair?.classList.add("hidden");
  }
  function resize() {
    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;
    camera.updateProjectionMatrix();
  }

  return { scene, camera, update, enter, exit, resize };
}
```

```
import * as THREE from "three";
import { createPlanetView } from "./planetView.js";
import { createIslandView } from "./islandView.js";

const canvas = document.getElementById("scene");
const renderer = new THREE.WebGLRenderer({ canvas, antialias: true });
renderer.setSize(window.innerWidth, window.innerHeight);
renderer.setPixelRatio(Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio, 2));
renderer.toneMapping = THREE.ACESFilmicToneMapping;
renderer.toneMappingExposure = 0.92;
renderer.outputColorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;

// ---------- level-one memory workbench ----------
const panel = document.getElementById("panel");
const panelTitle = document.getElementById("panel-title");
const panelBody = document.getElementById("panel-body");
const panelFoot = document.getElementById("panel-foot");
const aiLine = document.getElementById("ai-line");
const stabilityPips = document.getElementById("stability-pips");
const stabilityCount = document.getElementById("stability-count");
const memorySlot = document.getElementById("memory-slot");
const memoryOptions = document.getElementById("memory-options");
const memoryFeedback = document.getElementById("memory-feedback");
const terminalOutput = document.getElementById("terminal-output");
const beginReconstruction = document.getElementById("begin-reconstruction");
const commitMemory = document.getElementById("commit-memory");
const stagePanes = [...document.querySelectorAll("[data-pane]")];
const stepNodes = [...document.querySelectorAll("[data-step-node]")];

const WORKFLOW_STEPS = ["intro", "reconstruct", "diff", "commit", "complete"];
const MAX_STABILITY = 3;
const MEMORY_LINE = "Marked the gold island as the landing site before signal loss.";
const MEMORY_OPTIONS = [
  {
    text: MEMORY_LINE,
    correct: true,
    feedback: "Memory consistency check passed. The recovered line matches the helmet transcript.",
  },
  {
    text: "Diverted engine power to reopen the cargo bay before liftoff.",
    correct: false,
    feedback: "That contradicts the evidence. The helmet never mentions engines or cargo systems.",
  },
  {
    text: "Returned safely to orbit after the island survey completed.",
    correct: false,
    feedback: "That cannot be stable. The transcript ends before any return to orbit.",
  },
];
const AI_LINES = {
  intro: "I found evidence, but evidence is not memory. Help me reconstruct a stable record.",
  reconstruct: "Choose the line that belongs in the ship memory file. Wrong context weakens the buffer.",
  diff: "Good. Now review the actual change before it becomes part of history.",
  commit: "The commit is being written. Watch how the checkpoint is created around committed work.",
  complete: "Checkpoint sealed. I can now recover the commit, intent, and transcript later.",
};
const COMMIT_LINES = [
  '$ git commit -m "Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory"',
  "",
  "[main a17c9e2] Restore EVA-03 landing-site memory",
  " 1 file changed, 1 insertion(+), 1 deletion(-)",
  "",
  "$ entire hooks git post-commit",
  "condensing active session transcript...",
  "linking commit a17c9e2 to checkpoint 9f42c8a1d6b3",
  "",
  "Entire-Checkpoint: 9f42c8a1d6b3",
];

// The artifact context for the panel that's currently open. islandView hands us
// the fragment plus how to mark it checkpointed and how to read overall progress.
let panelCtx = null;
let stability = MAX_STABILITY;
let currentStep = "intro";
let commitTimers = [];

function renderFoot(progress) {
  if (!progress) { panelFoot.textContent = "find evidence · change memory · commit · checkpoint"; return; }
  const { recovered, total } = progress;
  panelFoot.textContent = recovered >= total
    ? "level complete · checkpointed memory restored"
    : `checkpointed memories · ${recovered}/${total}`;
}

function clearCommitTimers() {
  for (const timer of commitTimers) clearTimeout(timer);
  commitTimers = [];
}

function renderStability() {
  stabilityPips.innerHTML = "";
  for (let i = 0; i < MAX_STABILITY; i++) {
    const pip = document.createElement("span");
    pip.className = i < stability ? "is-live" : "is-lost";
    stabilityPips.append(pip);
  }
  stabilityCount.textContent = `${stability}/${MAX_STABILITY}`;
}

function setStep(step) {
  currentStep = step;
  panel.dataset.step = step;
  stagePanes.forEach((pane) => {
    pane.classList.toggle("hidden", pane.dataset.pane !== step);
  });

  const currentIndex = WORKFLOW_STEPS.indexOf(step);
  stepNodes.forEach((node) => {
    const nodeIndex = WORKFLOW_STEPS.indexOf(node.dataset.stepNode);
    node.classList.toggle("is-active", nodeIndex === currentIndex);
    node.classList.toggle("is-done", nodeIndex < currentIndex);
  });

  aiLine.textContent = AI_LINES[step];
}

function renderOptions() {
  memoryOptions.innerHTML = "";
  for (const option of MEMORY_OPTIONS) {
    const button = document.createElement("button");
    button.type = "button";
    button.className = "memory-choice";
    button.textContent = option.text;
    button.addEventListener("click", () => chooseMemory(option, button));
    memoryOptions.append(button);
  }
}

function resetPuzzle() {
  stability = MAX_STABILITY;
  memorySlot.textContent = "[MISSING]";
  memorySlot.classList.remove("is-filled", "is-error");
  memoryFeedback.textContent = "";
  memoryFeedback.className = "memory-feedback";
  renderStability();
  renderOptions();
}

function chooseMemory(option, button) {
  if (currentStep !== "reconstruct") return;
  memorySlot.textContent = option.text;
  memorySlot.classList.toggle("is-filled", option.correct);
  memorySlot.classList.toggle("is-error", !option.correct);
  memoryFeedback.textContent = option.feedback;
  memoryFeedback.className = `memory-feedback ${option.correct ? "is-good" : "is-bad"}`;

  if (option.correct) {
    [...memoryOptions.children].forEach((child) => { child.disabled = true; });
    button.classList.add("is-selected");
    setTimeout(() => setStep("diff"), 850);
    return;
  }

  stability -= 1;
  renderStability();
  button.disabled = true;
  button.classList.add("is-wrong");

  if (stability <= 0) {
    memoryFeedback.textContent = "Context buffer collapsed. Restoring the last stable state...";
    memoryFeedback.className = "memory-feedback is-bad is-collapse";
    [...memoryOptions.children].forEach((child) => { child.disabled = true; });
    setTimeout(resetPuzzle, 1200);
  }
}

function playCommitSequence() {
  clearCommitTimers();
  terminalOutput.textContent = "";
  setStep("commit");

  COMMIT_LINES.forEach((line, index) => {
    const timer = setTimeout(() => {
      terminalOutput.textContent += `${line}\n`;
      terminalOutput.scrollTop = terminalOutput.scrollHeight;
      if (index === COMMIT_LINES.length - 1) {
        commitTimers.push(setTimeout(completeCheckpoint, 700));
      }
    }, index * 360);
    commitTimers.push(timer);
  });
}

function completeCheckpoint() {
  clearCommitTimers();
  const progress = panelCtx?.onComplete?.();
  panelCtx.checkpointed = true;
  renderFoot(progress);
  setStep("complete");
}

function openPanel(frag, ctx = {}) {
  clearCommitTimers();
  panelCtx = ctx;
  panelTitle.textContent = frag.title;
  panelBody.textContent = "Recover the missing context from this helmet logger, commit the memory file, and let Entire create the checkpoint tied to that work.";
  resetPuzzle();
  renderFoot(ctx.progress);
  setStep(ctx.checkpointed ? "complete" : "intro");
  panel.classList.remove("hidden");
}
function closePanel() {
  clearCommitTimers();
  panel.classList.add("hidden");
}

beginReconstruction?.addEventListener("click", () => setStep("reconstruct"));
commitMemory?.addEventListener("click", playCommitSequence);
document.getElementById("panel-close").addEventListener("click", closePanel);
window.addEventListener("keydown", (e) => { if (e.key === "Escape") closePanel(); });

// ---------- background music ----------
const bgm = document.getElementById("bgm");
const audioPanel = document.getElementById("audio-panel");
const audioToggle = document.getElementById("audio-toggle");
const audioMute = document.getElementById("audio-mute");
const audioVolume = document.getElementById("audio-volume");
let userPausedMusic = false;

if (bgm && audioPanel && audioToggle && audioMute && audioVolume) {
  bgm.volume = Number(audioVolume.value);
  bgm.autoplay = true;
  bgm.loop = true;

  const updateAudioUi = () => {
    const playing = !bgm.paused;
    const awaitingStart = !playing && !userPausedMusic;
    audioPanel.classList.toggle("is-awaiting-start", awaitingStart);
    audioToggle.classList.toggle("is-playing", playing);
    audioToggle.setAttribute("aria-label", playing ? "Pause music" : "Start music");
    audioToggle.title = playing ? "Pause music" : "Start music";
    audioMute.classList.toggle("is-muted", bgm.muted || bgm.volume === 0);
    audioMute.setAttribute("aria-label", bgm.muted ? "Unmute music" : "Mute music");
    audioMute.title = bgm.muted ? "Unmute music" : "Mute music";
  };

  const tryPlayMusic = () => {
    if (userPausedMusic) return;
    bgm.play().then(updateAudioUi).catch(updateAudioUi);
  };
```

```
import * as THREE from "three";
import { createTerrain } from "./terrain.js";
import { createFirstPerson } from "./firstPerson.js";
import { FRAGMENTS, BUILDERS } from "./debris.js";

// The walkable island level. First-person: you find the EVA helmet and complete
// the first checkpoint lesson: evidence -> memory edit -> commit -> checkpoint.

const INTERACT_DIST = 7;   // how close (world units, XZ) to inspect an artifact
const BEAM_COLOR = 0xffd27a; // warm gold to match the planet's islands

// First level is intentionally one artifact so the checkpoint flow is clear.
const LEVEL_FRAGMENTS = FRAGMENTS.filter((frag) => frag.kind === "helmet");

// Place the helmet down the sightline from the spawn point.
const PLACEMENTS = [
  { x: 0, z: 32 },
];

function makeBeamTexture() {
  const c = document.createElement("canvas");
  c.width = 8; c.height = 128;
  const ctx = c.getContext("2d");
  const g = ctx.createLinearGradient(0, 0, 0, 128);
  g.addColorStop(0, "rgba(255,210,122,0)");
  g.addColorStop(1, "rgba(255,210,122,0.9)");
  ctx.fillStyle = g;
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, 8, 128);
  const t = new THREE.CanvasTexture(c);
  t.colorSpace = THREE.SRGBColorSpace;
  return t;
}

function makeMemoryAura() {
  const group = new THREE.Group();
  const cyan = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({
    color: 0x6fe3ff,
    transparent: true,
    opacity: 0.42,
    blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending,
    depthWrite: false,
  });
  const gold = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({
    color: 0xffd27a,
    transparent: true,
    opacity: 0.5,
    blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending,
    depthWrite: false,
  });

  const ringA = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.TorusGeometry(1.4, 0.018, 8, 80), cyan);
  const ringB = new THREE.Mesh(new THREE.TorusGeometry(1.95, 0.014, 8, 80), gold);
  ringA.rotation.x = Math.PI / 2;
  ringB.rotation.y = Math.PI / 2.8;
  group.add(ringA, ringB);

  const shardGeo = new THREE.TetrahedronGeometry(0.16, 0);
  for (let i = 0; i < 9; i++) {
    const shard = new THREE.Mesh(shardGeo, i % 2 ? cyan.clone() : gold.clone());
    const angle = (i / 9) * Math.PI * 2;
    const radius = 1.3 + (i % 3) * 0.24;
    shard.position.set(Math.cos(angle) * radius, 0.2 + (i % 4) * 0.28, Math.sin(angle) * radius);
    shard.rotation.set(i * 0.7, angle, i * 0.33);
    group.add(shard);
  }

  group.position.y = 2.65;
  return group;
}

export function createIslandView(renderer, { openPanel, onExit } = {}) {
  const canvas = renderer.domElement;

  // ---------- scene & sky ----------
  const scene = new THREE.Scene();
  scene.background = new THREE.Color(0x2a2350); // lavender dusk sky
  scene.fog = new THREE.Fog(0x2a2350, 90, 320);

  const camera = new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(
    62, window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight, 0.1, 2000
  );

  // ---------- lighting (soft, warm sun in a lavender sky) ----------
  const hemi = new THREE.HemisphereLight(0xcdbcff, 0x3a2f5e, 0.7);
  scene.add(hemi);
  const sun = new THREE.DirectionalLight(0xfff1dc, 1.5);
  sun.position.set(60, 90, 40);
  scene.add(sun);
  scene.add(new THREE.AmbientLight(0x6a5a92, 0.3));

  // Big sky dome so the horizon isn't an empty void.
  const dome = new THREE.Mesh(
    new THREE.SphereGeometry(900, 32, 16),
    new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: 0x3a3168, side: THREE.BackSide, fog: false })
  );
  scene.add(dome);

  // ---------- terrain ----------
  const terrain = createTerrain({ size: 200, segments: 220, maxHeight: 26, seed: 1337 });
  scene.add(terrain.mesh, terrain.water);

  // ---------- artifacts ----------
  const beamTex = makeBeamTexture();
  const artifacts = [];
  LEVEL_FRAGMENTS.forEach((frag, idx) => {
    const place = PLACEMENTS[idx % PLACEMENTS.length];
    const groundY = terrain.heightAt(place.x, place.z);
    const anchor = new THREE.Group();
    anchor.position.set(place.x, groundY, place.z);

    const model = BUILDERS[frag.kind]();
    model.scale.setScalar(14); // the props are planet-scale tiny; size up for FP
    model.position.y = 1.4;
    anchor.add(model);

    // A tall light beam so the artifact is findable from across the island.
    const beam = new THREE.Mesh(
      new THREE.CylinderGeometry(0.6, 0.6, 60, 12, 1, true),
      new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({
        map: beamTex, color: BEAM_COLOR, transparent: true, opacity: 0.5,
        blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending, depthWrite: false, side: THREE.DoubleSide,
        fog: false,
      })
    );
    beam.position.y = 30;
    anchor.add(beam);

    const glow = new THREE.PointLight(BEAM_COLOR, 8, 40, 2);
    glow.position.y = 3;
    anchor.add(glow);

    const aura = makeMemoryAura();
    anchor.add(aura);

    scene.add(anchor);
    artifacts.push({ anchor, model, beam, aura, fragment: frag, collected: false });
  });

  // ---------- first-person controller ----------
  const fp = createFirstPerson(camera, canvas, {
    heightAt: terrain.heightAt,
    radius: terrain.radius * 0.96,
    eyeHeight: 2.6,
    speed: 24,
  });
  scene.add(fp.controls.object);

  // ---------- HUD elements ----------
  const promptEl = document.getElementById("fp-prompt");
  const controlsEl = document.getElementById("fp-controls");
  const crosshair = document.getElementById("crosshair");
  const islandHud = document.getElementById("island-hud");

  let target = null;     // nearest artifact in range
  let active = false;    // is this view the one being shown?
  let promptText = null;
  let controlsLocked = null;

  // How many core memories have been checkpointed (restored), out of the total.
  const progressNow = () => ({
    recovered: artifacts.filter((a) => a.collected).length,
    total: artifacts.length,
  });

  function setPrompt(text) {
    if (!promptEl) return;
    if (text === promptText) return;
    promptText = text;
    if (text) { promptEl.textContent = text; promptEl.classList.remove("hidden"); }
    else promptEl.classList.add("hidden");
  }
  function setControls(locked) {
    if (!controlsEl) return;
    if (locked === controlsLocked && !controlsEl.classList.contains("hidden")) return;
    controlsLocked = locked;
    controlsEl.innerHTML = `
      <span class="control-item">
        <span class="control-label">To move</span>
        <span class="arrow-keys" aria-label="Arrow keys">
          <span class="key key-up">↑</span>
          <span class="key key-left">←</span>
          <span class="key key-down">↓</span>
          <span class="key key-right">→</span>
        </span>
      </span>
      ${locked ? `
        <span class="control-item">
          <span class="control-label">To look around</span>
          <span class="mouse-hint">
            <span class="mouse-icon" aria-hidden="true"></span>
            <span>move mouse</span>
          </span>
        </span>
        <span class="control-item">
          <span class="control-label">Free cursor</span>
          <span class="key key-wide">Esc</span>
        </span>
      ` : ""}
      <span class="control-item">
        <span class="control-label">Return to orbit</span>
        <span class="key">B</span>
      </span>
    `;
    controlsEl.classList.remove("hidden");
  }
  function hideControls() {
    controlsLocked = null;
    controlsEl?.classList.add("hidden");
  }

  // ---------- input ----------
  function onCanvasClick() {
    if (active && !fp.isLocked) fp.lock();
  }
  function onKeyDown(e) {
    if (!active) return;
    if (e.code === "Escape" && fp.isLocked) fp.unlock();
    if (e.code === "KeyE" && target) {
      fp.unlock();              // free the mouse for the panel
      const a = target;         // inspecting starts the checkpoint lesson; it
      openPanel?.(a.fragment, { //   is not saved until the commit flow finishes.
        checkpointed: a.collected,
        progress: progressNow(),
        onComplete: () => { a.collected = true; return progressNow(); },
      });
    }
    if (e.code === "KeyB") onExit?.(); // leave the island, back to orbit
  }
  // Show the crosshair only while the mouse is captured.
  fp.controls.addEventListener("lock", () => crosshair?.classList.remove("hidden"));
  fp.controls.addEventListener("unlock", () => crosshair?.classList.add("hidden"));

  // ---------- per-frame ----------
  const clockOffset = Math.random() * 10;
  function update(dt, t) {
    fp.update(dt);

    // Find the nearest artifact within reach (ignoring height).
    let nearest = null, nearestD = Infinity;
    for (const a of artifacts) {
      const dx = camera.position.x - a.anchor.position.x;
      const dz = camera.position.z - a.anchor.position.z;
      const d = Math.hypot(dx, dz);
      if (d < nearestD) { nearestD = d; nearest = a; }
      // Idle spin + bob on the prop.
      a.model.rotation.y += dt * 0.6;
      a.model.position.y = 1.4 + Math.sin((t + clockOffset) * 1.5 + a.anchor.position.x) * 0.4;
      a.beam.material.opacity = a.collected ? 0.12 : 0.4 + Math.sin(t * 2) * 0.12;
      a.aura.rotation.y += dt * (a.collected ? 0.35 : 0.95);
      a.aura.rotation.x = Math.sin(t * 0.7) * 0.18;
      a.aura.traverse((child) => {
        if (child.material) child.material.opacity = a.collected
          ? 0.14
          : 0.35 + Math.sin(t * 3 + child.id) * 0.12;
      });
    }
    target = nearestD <= INTERACT_DIST ? nearest : null;

    if (active) {
      setControls(fp.isLocked);
      if (!fp.isLocked) setPrompt("Click to look around");
      else if (target) {
        setPrompt(`Press E to reconstruct · ${target.fragment.title}` +
          (target.collected ? "  · ✓ checkpointed" : ""));
      } else setPrompt("Find the helmet memory beacon");
    }
  }

  // ---------- lifecycle ----------
  function enter() {
    active = true;
    fp.attach();
    // Drop the player near the south shore, looking toward the centre.
    fp.groundAt(0, terrain.radius * 0.75);
    camera.lookAt(0, terrain.heightAt(0, 0) + 6, 0);
    canvas.addEventListener("click", onCanvasClick);
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
    islandHud?.classList.remove("hidden");
    setControls(false);
    crosshair?.classList.add("hidden");
  }
  function exit() {
    active = false;
    fp.unlock();
    fp.detach();
    canvas.removeEventListener("click", onCanvasClick);
    window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKeyDown);
    setPrompt(null);
    hideControls();
    islandHud?.classList.add("hidden");
    crosshair?.classList.add("hidden");
  }
  function resize() {
    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;
    camera.updateProjectionMatrix();
  }

  return { scene, camera, update, enter, exit, resize };
}

```

> AGENT

Good catch from the screenshot: the workbench was visible on load because `#panel { display: grid }` beat the global `.hidden` class. I’m fixing that with an explicit `#panel.hidden { display: none; }`. The screenshot did confirm the workbench styling itself is rendering, but it should only appear after inspecting the helmet.

```json
{
  "cmd": "node --input-type=module <<'NODE'\nconst tabs = await fetch('http://127.0.0.1:9223/json/list').then((r) => r.json());\nconst tab = tabs.find((t) => t.type === 'page') || tabs[0];\nasync function run(duration) {\n  const ws = new WebSocket(tab.webSocketDebuggerUrl);\n  let id = 0;\n  const pending = new Map();\n  ws.addEventListener('message', (event) => {\n    const msg = JSON.parse(event.data);\n    if (msg.id && pending.has(msg.id)) { pending.get(msg.id)(msg); pending.delete(msg.id); }\n  });\n  await new Promise((resolve) => ws.addEventListener('open', resolve, { once: true }));\n  const send = (method, params = {}) => new Promise((resolve) => { const msgId = ++id; pending.set(msgId, resolve); ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id: msgId, method, params })); });\n  const wait = (ms) => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));\n  await send('Page.enable'); await send('Runtime.enable');\n  await send('Page.navigate', { url: 'http://127.0.0.1:5173/?view=island' });\n  await wait(1800);\n  await send('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: `Object.defineProperty(document, 'pointerLockElement', { configurable: true, get: () => document.getElementById('scene') }); document.dispatchEvent(new Event('pointerlockchange'));` });\n  await send('Input.dispatchKeyEvent', { type: 'keyDown', key: 'w', code: 'KeyW', windowsVirtualKeyCode: 87, nativeVirtualKeyCode: 87 });\n  await wait(duration);\n  await send('Input.dispatchKeyEvent', { type: 'keyUp', key: 'w', code: 'KeyW', windowsVirtualKeyCode: 87, nativeVirtualKeyCode: 87 });\n  await wait(400);\n  const prompt = await send('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: 'document.querySelector(\"#fp-prompt\")?.textContent', returnByValue: true });\n  ws.close();\n  return { duration, prompt: prompt.result.result.value };\n}\nconst results = [];\nfor (const duration of [900, 1100, 1300, 1500]) results.push(await run(duration));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(results, null, 2));\nNODE",
```

```json
{
  "cmd": "node --input-type=module <<'NODE'\nconst tabs = await fetch('http://127.0.0.1:9223/json/list').then((r) => r.json());\nconst tab = tabs.find((t) => t.type === 'page') || tabs[0];\nconst ws = new WebSocket(tab.webSocketDebuggerUrl);\nlet id = 0;\nconst pending = new Map();\nws.addEventListener('message', (event) => { const msg = JSON.parse(event.data); if (msg.id && pending.has(msg.id)) { pending.get(msg.id)(msg); pending.delete(msg.id); } });\nawait new Promise((resolve) => ws.addEventListener('open', resolve, { once: true }));\nconst send = (method, params = {}) => new Promise((resolve) => { const msgId = ++id; pending.set(msgId, resolve); ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id: msgId, method, params })); });\nawait new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 6000));\nconst state = await send('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: '({step: document.querySelector(\"#panel\").dataset.step, terminalText: document.querySelector(\"#terminal-output\").textContent, footer: document.querySelector(\"#panel-foot\").textContent})', returnByValue: true });\nws.close();\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(state.result.result.value, null, 2));\nNODE",
```

> AGENT

Implemented the first-level checkpoint flow and made it much more parallel to real Entire usage.

What changed:
- The island is now focused on one first-level artifact: the EVA helmet memory beacon in [src/islandView.js](/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/planetfall-seed-signalkit/src/islandView.js:7).
- Inspecting it opens a full “ship memory workbench” instead of a simple checkpoint button in [index.html](/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/planetfall-seed-signalkit/index.html:63).
- The level now teaches: evidence → memory edit → diff → commit → Entire checkpoint in [src/main.js](/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/planetfall-seed-signalkit/src/main.js:13).
- Added the graphic-heavy workbench UI: animated memory field, stability meter, step rail, file editor, diff, terminal output, checkpoint record, and responsive styling in [src/style.css](/Users/goose-guest/Documents/work/planetfall-seed-signalkit/src/style.css:172).
- Added an in-world cyan/gold memory aura around the helmet so the objective reads more clearly.

How it plays:
Open `http://127.0.0.1:5173/`, land on the island, or jump straight to `http://127.0.0.1:5173/?view=island`. Click to look, walk to the helmet beacon, press `E`, choose the recovered memory line, review the diff, commit it, then the checkpoint record appears.

Verified:
- `npm run build` passes.
- Local server returns `200`.
- Headless Chrome/CDP test reached the helmet, opened the panel, confirmed wrong answers reduce stability, confirmed the correct answer advances to diff, and confirmed the commit output eventually creates `Entire-Checkpoint: 9f42c8a1d6b3`.

The dev server is still running at `http://127.0.0.1:5173/`.

> DEVELOPER

i played the game. i do not get it. like when i had to reconstruct the memory. i had no understanding of why that would be the memory reconstruction. it was super confusing. can you brainstorm with me before you make any changes

> AGENT

Yeah, that makes sense. The confusing part is probably this: the game asked you to choose a sentence, but it didn’t give you enough reason to believe that sentence was “the memory.” It felt like guessing the author’s intended lore line.

I think the first level needs a more literal chain of evidence.

Better shape:

**Evidence should be copyable, not interpretive.**

Instead of:

```txt
Evidence:
gold ridge visible
landing pin confirmed
signal loss in 00:14

Choose:
Marked the gold island as the landing site before signal loss.
```

That asks the player to synthesize a sentence. Too abstract.

Maybe it should be:

```txt
helmet/raw-transcript.log

00:03 suit camera: GOLD RIDGE VISIBLE
00:07 nav overlay: LANDING SITE CONFIRMED
00:14 comms: SIGNAL LOST
```

Then the corrupted memory file is:

```txt
ship-memory/eva-03.log

00:03 suit camera: GOLD RIDGE VISIBLE
00:07 nav overlay: [MISSING]
00:14 comms: SIGNAL LOST
```

And the player drags/selects:

```txt
LANDING SITE CONFIRMED
```

Now it is obvious why that is reconstruction: you are restoring an exact missing transcript line.

Then after that, the real Entire-parallel lesson starts:

```txt
diff:
- 00:07 nav overlay: [MISSING]
+ 00:07 nav overlay: LANDING SITE CONFIRMED

commit:
Restore missing EVA-03 nav overlay line

Entire-Checkpoint: 9f42c8a1d6b3
```

That’s much clearer because the puzzle is not “understand the lore.” It is “restore the missing line from evidence.”

A few possible directions:

1. **Transcript Patch**
   Player patches one missing line from the helmet transcript into the ship memory file. Most parallel to code diff.

2. **Structured Memory Record**
   Player fills fields:
   `site=gold_island`, `marker=confirmed`, `signal_loss=00:14`.
   Very clear, less poetic.

3. **Scan Then Auto-Reconstruct**
   Player clicks/collects three evidence shards around the helmet, then the AI proposes the memory line. Player only approves the diff/commit. More cinematic, less puzzle.

4. **Commit-First Tutorial**
   The memory is already reconstructed. Level one only teaches that after a change, you commit, then Entire creates a checkpoint. Simplest and probably best for onboarding.

My instinct: use **Transcript Patch**. It is game-like, but nobody has to guess what “memory reconstruction” means. The player literally repairs a missing line in a log, reviews a diff, commits it, and sees the checkpoint form.

> DEVELOPER

why would ladning site confirmed be the answer..like i dont know am i missing some kind of intuition

> AGENT

No, you’re not missing intuition. The game is missing the bridge.

Right now “landing site confirmed” is only the answer because I, as designer, knew the intended sentence. From the player side, it’s arbitrary. You see some vibe-clues, then you’re asked to pick a memory line. That’s not reconstruction; that’s guessing the writer’s lore.

For this to work, the player needs an objective reason.

Something like:

```txt
HELMET RAW LOG
00:03 camera      GOLD RIDGE VISIBLE
00:07 nav         LANDING SITE CONFIRMED
00:14 comms       SIGNAL LOST
```

and beside it:

```txt
SHIP MEMORY FILE
00:03 camera      GOLD RIDGE VISIBLE
00:07 nav         [MISSING]
00:14 comms       SIGNAL LOST
```

Then `LANDING SITE CONFIRMED` is the answer because:

- the missing slot is timestamp `00:07`
- the source is `nav`
- the helmet raw log has exactly one `00:07 nav` line
- the player is copying/restoring evidence, not interpreting it

That’s the missing intuition. The player should be thinking:

> “Oh, this memory file lost a line. I found the matching line in the helmet log. I’ll patch it in.”

Not:

> “Which of these sentences sounds like what the game wants?”

This also makes it more parallel to coding. In code, you usually don’t make a change because of vibes. You saw a failing case, a diff, a missing value, a broken line. The change has evidence behind it.

I think the fix conceptually is:

**Level one should not ask the player to synthesize a memory. It should ask them to restore an exact missing record.**

Even clearer:

```txt
Missing memory:
[00:07] nav.overlay = ???

Recovered evidence:
[00:07] nav.overlay = LANDING_SITE_CONFIRMED
```

Then the diff becomes obvious:

```diff
- [00:07] nav.overlay = MISSING
+ [00:07] nav.overlay = LANDING_SITE_CONFIRMED
```

So yeah: the current version is too abstract. The next version should make the “why” visible through timestamp, source label, and exact text matching.

I’ll read the docs Quick Start and recalibrate around your actual workflow before suggesting changes.
